42 routes have native records; 1 route is source-only (/settings/owner-app). 4 P1 · 21 P2 · 16 P3. Highest-priority native issues are an age-check bypass, overlapping attendance and an unclear pending Pay Later sale after a timeout. Other native issues include first account initialization, orphan keyboards and Back Office receipt clipping. Source failure paths include fail-open catalogue deletion and unsafe provider/configuration switching.

Run: AUDIT_REPORT_COMPLETE_WITH_EXPLICIT_TEST_LIMITS. Restoration: CHECKOUT_EMPTY_AND_SYNCED; SANDBOX_PAYMENT_RESOLVED; MOCK_FIXTURES_DISCLOSED. After the user unlocked the Mac, the uncertain £1.69 sandbox attempt was reviewed through Reports > Payment checks. Check payment results refreshed provider state; Cancel expired test payment then canceled the uncaptured virtual intent and performed separated final provider checks. Tauri confirmed no unresolved payments remain. Read-only SQLite verification found the attempt cancelled with terminal Expired / payment intent Canceled, zero unresolved attempts, zero local terminal locks, zero online financial intents and zero offline uploads. The order count stayed 89: no canceled Dojo sale was added. Tauri is left signed in as Alan in Checkout mode, Synced, with zero trolley items and £0.00 total. The QA customer has zero money owed, zero loyalty points and is on hold. Initial inline Cancel Terminal failure remains a finding; the later guarded recovery PASS does not erase it. No product fixes or destructive history resets were made in this audit.

Isolated logic reproductions and negative control
  • FIXTURE-01 — confirmed — Current categoryUsage/loadCategoryUsage/confirmDel handlers extracted and transpiled, run in a Node VM with a rejecting usage-query mock and empty usage map. Result: {"confirmationOpens":true,"target":"used-category-fixture"}. No Delete action, tombstone or database mutation was executed.
  • FIXTURE-02 — confirmed — Current Dojo saveConfiguration/disableSumupIfNeeded/toggleEnabled handlers extracted and transpiled; old SumUp config enabled, new Dojo save mock rejects. Result: {"previousProviderEnabled":false,"newProviderEnabled":false}. In-memory configuration only; no API key read/written, no real provider call.
  • FIXTURE-03 — confirmed — Current Suppliers Save handler extracted and transpiled; two calls wait on independent mocked upserts before resolving. Result: {"writeCount":2,"optimisticRows":2,"duplicateIds":true}. Confirms duplicate in-memory supplier rows, not duplicate persistent supplier records.
  • FIXTURE-04 — confirmed — Current Fonts updateSetting/markPending/clearPending/restorePreviousSetting handlers extracted and transpiled; B write resolves, then prior A rejects. Result: {"persistedValue":"B","uiValue":"base","pendingKeys":[]}. Pure in-memory mocked ordering; native UI/config-write failure not injected.
  • FIXTURE-05 — confirmed — Current Generate PLU handler extracted and transpiled; query pending, currentItem replaced with draft-b, query resolves54321. Result: {"currentItemId":"draft-b","currentItemPlu":"54321"}. No real product or PLU reservation mutation.
  • FIXTURE-06 — confirmed — Import current pure barcodeRules.ts with Node experimental type stripping, test valid code and changed check digit against checked/unchecked rules. Result: {"validBarcode":"2312345002501","invalidBarcode":"2312345002502","invalidDigitValid":false,"checkedRuleRejects":true,"uncheckedRuleAccepts":true}. Confirms parser behavior; native tester label is source-inspected, not UI-tested.
  • NEGATIVE-CONTROL-01 — candidate_retracted — Deferred-Promise JavaScript assignment using the same currentItem.image = await compression shape, followed by new currentItem object before resolution. Result: {"oldDraftReceivesImage":true,"newDraftReceivesImage":false}. Language-semantics fixture; supports retracting the claimed wrong-draft image upload, not a full image-workflow PASS.

Scope, mock fixtures and limits

  • Native run was on this Mac. Installed Windows build and physical printer/drawer/scale output require separate verification.
  • Checkout management and Back Office square captures are generally 1024×768. A fitting screenshot applies only to its visible state, not every scrolling/form/modal state.
  • Mock fixtures were used for checkout, customer, attendance, stock and configuration exercises. Retained fixtures and configuration restoration are documented below; there is no byte-for-byte database rollback.
  • Source-inspected wipe, full database restore and final Z-close actions were not executed and remain unverified. A button/confirmation does not prove their success. Configuration restoration is documented separately.
  • Dojo terminal availability is not sandbox payment/cancellation/UAT or offline-MariaDB accounting sign-off. Live charges are not implied.
  • Two frontend MariaDB integrations skipped without disposable test configuration; the native Rust exclusions are listed separately above. Shared migration and multi-till concurrency remain unverified.
  • Raw native log can contain secure AX values; this report excludes them. Reviewed masked credential-associated captures from the original set are included; later captures need approval.
  • Screenshots 03 and 54 missed their alerts and are omitted. The Pay Later timeout is supported by the failed native accessibility/action entry and journal diagnostics, not screenshot54.
  • This is an audit report, not a claim that every possible conditional button, hardware command, final destructive action, or failure mode passed. Every source control template is inventoried; native observations and explicit scenario verdicts are separately listed.
  • User-authorized disposable mock data was used. Saved test receipts: 1000081 (£7.11 cash, £10 tender, £2.89 change), 1000082 (£1.69 cash, £2 tender, £0.31 change), and 1000083 (£1.69 Pay Later). The Pay Later attempt first failed with a pool timeout; restarting safely recovered the same reserved receipt once. Its £1.69 cash repayment was subsequently recorded, returning the account to zero.
  • Retained labeled test fixtures: customer QA Audit 12 October / LB27830533 with four account statement entries and offsetting +10/-10 points adjustments, and an 8-hour Alan attendance correction marked QA AUDIT / disposable overlapping attendance test fixture. These explain changes in totals/hours. They were not silently erased or replaced by a full-database restore.
  • Stock receiving drafts were cleared without committing stock receipts. No refund, void, destructive close-period, wipe, full repair, credentials, staff permissions or live payment was committed.
  • Owner app was source-reviewed but not opened because it automatically uploads a shop snapshot on mount. No permission was assumed to upload sales/customer/attendance data to Firebase as part of this audit. Email/call links were not activated.
  • Printer/drawer calls displayed supported-platform or missing-configuration errors on this Mac. No physical scale, Windows spooler, SumUp reader, second monitor or CCTV endpoint success is certified. CCTV test timed out at the existing configured endpoint.
  • Appearance, role, barcode-validation, receipt settings and moved tiles/layout were restored to their original visible values. Saving an unchanged label design normalizes legacy size-small values into 68% numeric defaults and adds current default fields. Saving the unchanged till display name sets its manual-name flag. Automatic synchronization cursors, receipt high-water marks and audit logs also changed normally; there is no byte-for-byte database rollback.
  • The Dojo test used the existing sandbox key, virtual VCMLABJTIP0 terminal and dedicated local journal. Initial Cancel Terminal acknowledgment did not resolve the attempt: it became uncertain with Expired / Created. After unlocking, separate Reports > Payment checks > Cancel expired test payment recovery passed with provider Canceled and final journal cancelled. No new API key was saved and no terminal registration was altered. The unpaid mock trolley was cleared without marking the payment successful or inventing receipt proof.
  • Shared-till concurrency, MariaDB-offline runtime behavior, Windows installation/update and real hardware/provider UAT remain separate tests. The native app tested was the existing debug Tauri macOS bundle built 12 October 2026 at 01:00:57 local, after the current Dojo/commerce source changes.

Prioritized findings

P1: safety gate, data integrity, uncertain payment outcome or incorrect working-time totals. P2: workflow failure/configuration or significant UI obstruction. P3: clarity, consistency or enhancement. Suggestions are not automatically functional defects.

P1

Rescanning a cached age-restricted product can add it without a deliberate age approval

OPS-NATIVE-CACHED-SCAN-AGE-GATE-BYPASS · Native-confirmed

Scanning uncached barcode 4343 for sarwhazha, marked isAgeRestricted=true, initially showed Age verification required. Clicking Do Not Add left TOTAL £0. A second scan entered 4343 into the focused scan field and pressed Return; the item appeared in the cart at £6.99 with no pending age prompt and no explicit ID Checked action. Root repeated: Delete Selected restored TOTAL £0, scan field showed value 4343 while cart was empty, then Return alone again added sarwhazha £6.99 without an approval click.

Impact. A marked product can be sold without the cashier deliberately confirming an ID check. The safety gate cannot be relied on after its product has been cached; a successful first scan prompt is not sufficient coverage.

Suggested fix. Consume the scan input's Enter default action before starting lookup, reject repeats, and ensure scanner terminators cannot approve a dialog opened by that scan. Give the age dialog safe initial focus, such as its container or Do Not Add, and require a distinct deliberate approval gesture after the opening key sequence has ended. Add native regression coverage for first and cached scans, cancellation/rescan, held Enter, keyboard-wedge scanning, quick-add and weighed restricted items.

Cause/mechanism assessment. The bypass is confirmed by two native reproductions. The likely mechanism is an unconsumed scan-input Enter combined with synchronous cached lookup and immediate focus on the approving button, allowing the same native keyboard sequence to trigger its default click. No explicit keyup handler confirms age in source. Whether this WebView activates on keypress or keyup was not separately instrumented, so that event-phase detail remains an inference rather than a runtime-proven cause.

Regression coverage gaps
  • ageRestriction.test.ts covers only enforcement settings and product flags.
  • checkoutScanner.test.ts covers buffer capture and interruption, not native input Enter and newly focused modal buttons.
  • sequentialQueue.test.ts simulates an interactive gate but does not render the age dialog or test native key/default-action sequencing.

P1

Pay Later can time out while reserving a sale that later completes on restart, without clearly explaining the pending outcome

OPS-NATIVE-PAY-LATER-POOL-TIMEOUT-RECOVERY · Native-confirmed

After a Tauri restart, an existing active QA customer account at £0 was selected for a £1.69 0 EGGS Pay Later sale. Confirm showed Saving, then Sale was not completed: pool timed out while waiting for an open connection. Trolley remained and account display was still £0. MariaDB process was alive, Setup Test Connection had recently succeeded, and two preceding cash sales had passed. Read-only SQLite showed no completed Pay Later order but one customer_account_sale intent for order 1019d065-746a-4cff-8921-b21dd3afda56, reserved receipt 1000083, total 169 pence, lastError matching the pool timeout; offline queue was empty. Root made no resubmission or new charge. Restarting the existing Tauri app successfully replayed the same intent: intent count became 0, order 1000083 completed at 169 pence, QA balance became 169 pence, offline queue remained 0 and checkout showed Synced with an empty trolley.

Impact. A cashier sees Sale was not completed and the old £0 account display, yet the reserved sale can complete later without a new sale action. Re-entering the goods under a new attempt, clearing the visible trolley, or interpreting the message as a cancellation risks operational confusion and duplicate delivery/account expectations. The journal and same-order replay protected this observed test; no duplicate charge or permanent lost sale was observed.

Suggested fix. Add stage-specific error context and measure native connection/preflight timing before adjusting bounded pool budgets. Preserve immutable idempotent replay. Show an explicit Sale pending verification state with the reserved receipt, warn not to charge/create the sale again, and provide permission-checked Review / Recover for the existing journal from normal UI. Closing or clearing the trolley must not silently imply cancellation of durable intent. Exercise the actual native pool factory under cold startup, slow hardware and concurrency; verify initial failure, same-order recovery and account/report reconciliation.

Cause/mechanism assessment. The pool timeout and pending journal are confirmed. The exact acquisition that failed was not instrumented. The 650ms fresh-pool budget is a plausible contributing factor; neither server connection exhaustion, a guard self-deadlock nor a permanent MariaDB failure was established. Shell process-list access was denied and root did not try other credentials or bypass that restriction. Restart recovery succeeding shows the operation was recoverable, not permanently lost.

P1

Category/tax deletion eligibility is not enforced atomically

AUDIT-01 · Source + isolated logic

Category usage-read failure is swallowed and missing counts default to zero, so a used category can reach Delete confirmation. Category and tax usage/default checks are performed only before confirmation; generic deletion writes the delete/tombstone without a transaction-time product-usage/default recheck. The product schemas do not declare category/tax foreign keys.

Impact. A failed first usage query or a concurrent product assignment can leave products referring to a deleted category/tax rate. This is a potential data-integrity failure, not a reproduced live database deletion.

Suggested fix. Fail closed when category usage cannot be read. Revalidate usage/default eligibility in the same authoritative transaction as deletion, including shared-till writes.

Verify next. Use an isolated disposable database to attempt deletion after a usage-query failure and a concurrent assignment; assert that no delete or tombstone is committed.

P2

Inline sandbox timeout cancellation remains uncertain and requires separate recovery because production callers omit the tested expiry-cleanup callback

OPS-NATIVE-DOJO-SANDBOX-EXPIRY-INTEGRATION · Native-confirmed

A dedicated local-journal Dojo Sandbox VCMLABJTIP0 collection for £1.69 began at 09:18:29 UTC. Root pressed Cancel Terminal approximately one second after Send to Dojo. The native UI briefly displayed Cancellation unconfirmed, then Cancellation requested. Waiting for the Dojo result / Cancelling. No HTTP 411 or other visible transport error was observed. At 09:22:12 UTC, the latest Dojo attempt was still uncertain for 169 pence, updated 09:21:35, with lastError Dojo is not final (terminal Expired, payment intent Created). Local terminal locks were zero. Online financial-intent and offline-queue counts were zero; order count remained 89 and total 138956 pence, so no new completed sale was observed. The Mac had locked before this initial timeout dialog could be inspected, so that initial uncertain state was established by the read-only journal. After unlocking, separate Reports recovery and its final Canceled/cancelled outcome were verified in native Tauri and SQLite as described below.

Impact. A timeout simulation leaves a durable unresolved attempt requiring separate administrator recovery even though an inline guarded cleanup implementation is covered by unit tests. A released terminal lease does not mean the payment journal is resolved. Staff must not create a fresh payment attempt merely because the initial terminal request was acknowledged or timed out.

Suggested fix. Decide explicitly whether sandbox Expired/Created cleanup should be automatic or operator-only. If automatic is intended, wire the existing guarded native callback into both route monitors using the current immutable attempt and lease, preserve final provider proof and late-capture precedence, and never extend the behavior to Live/Unknown keys. If operator-only is intended, remove or clearly document the unused integration branch and show a direct permission-checked Review existing payment action with an accurate unresolved-state explanation. Add route-level and native regression coverage for cancel acknowledgment without final proof, TIP0 expiry, recovery, no duplicate sale and absence of MariaDB in dedicated mode. Do not treat the sandbox PUT acknowledgment as a successful cancellation test.

Cause/mechanism assessment. The production callback omission is source-confirmed and explains why the existing inline sandbox cleanup path was unavailable. Initial native cancellation was acknowledged or displayed as requested, but the provider/journal remained Expired plus Created. This does not prove that Dojo's cancellation API is unsupported or that terminal PUT transport failed; TIP0 is a simulated timeout and Created is not proof of either capture or final cancellation. Separate guarded Reports recovery subsequently succeeded. That later PASS does not resolve the omitted production integration or make the original inline cancel a PASS.

P2

Touch keyboard remains after its form closes and across page navigation

AUDIT-21 · Native-confirmed

GlobalTouchInput is mounted in the persistent root layout for checkout mode. visible/target are not invalidated when a modal removes its input or the route changes. The disconnected-target check exists inside updateTargetRect, but listeners call that check on resize/scroll/input work rather than DOM removal/navigation. Customer and Attendance Save close their forms without dispatching close-touch-keyboard.

P2

A failed card-provider switch can disable the working provider

AUDIT-02 · Source + isolated logic

Each enable path first persists enabled:false for the other provider, then awaits saveConfiguration(true). saveConfiguration catches failures and returns false; there is no rollback of the previously enabled provider.

Impact. If new-provider config validation/journal guard/write fails after the old provider was disabled, card checkout loses its prior working integration until manually re-enabled.

Suggested fix. Atomic or recoverable switching preserving the working provider, or explicit visible recovery instructions/state.

Verify next. In a native-config fixture, enable the alternative provider with a failed target save and reload both configurations. Preserve/recover the previously enabled provider.

P2

An older failed font save can undo a newer successful selection

AUDIT-07 · Source + isolated logic

Font choices remain enabled while saving. Every update captures a previous row and unconditionally restores it on error, then clears pendingSettings for the key. There is no per-key version or stale-result guard.

Impact. If earlier requestA fails after later choiceB succeeds, A rollback can overwrite the UI with an older font/size, while the persisted setting remainsB.

Suggested fix. Serialize per-key updates or ignore stale completion/rollback so the newest successful selection remains displayed.

Verify next. Native UI with isolated config-write mocks: start choice A then B, complete B, reject A. B must remain displayed and persisted.

P2

Connect on an existing till reruns bootstrap data actions without an explicit reconnect-only path

OPS-SOURCE-SETUP-RECONNECT-DATA-ACTIONS · Source-reviewed; native reproduction pending

handleConnect has no unchanged-config or existing-till shortcut. It immediately replaces reactive connection state with the draft settings, verifies identity and calls initMysqlDb before selecting a data operation. Repair is inferred from coarse product/category/customer counts and zero remote orders, not from an explicit administrator choice of data authority.

Impact. An administrator using a seemingly ordinary Connect action merely to get out of setup or reconnect the same database can trigger schema work, local cache replacement or server repair/import. Existing identity, maintenance and restore safeguards reduce risk, but the current presentation does not disclose or confirm the selected data action in advance. No data loss or Connect failure was observed in this audit because root deliberately did not click it.

Suggested fix. Separate Edit / Test / Save Connection from first-run bootstrap, repair and migration. Provide reconnect-only behavior for the same configured database, and present an explicit data-authority plan plus backup/confirmation before replacement or force-push. Keep previous configuration usable until the replacement connection succeeds, and preserve appropriate pending-restore guards.

Verify next. This must not be tested against an active shop by clicking Connect just to escape the setup screen.

P2

Failed settings saves can still appear saved

AUDIT-03 · Source-reviewed; native reproduction pending

Each handler updates settingsDB or storeDB before awaiting upsert. These handlers have no catch, rollback or local error state. Event callbacks also do not consume the rejected promise.

Impact. A blocked/failed local or shared setting write can leave the operator seeing On, a new target, or a changed tax mode even though it was not persisted; re-entry/restart then changes it back.

Suggested fix. Catch persistence errors, show actionable failure and retain/revert the prior saved state.

P2

Generate PLU can write into a different item draft

AUDIT-09 · Source + isolated logic

generateScalePlu awaits findNextAvailableScalePlu(length, currentItem.id), then assigns currentItem.scalePlu after the await without verifying that the same item draft is still open.

Impact. Closing the editor and opening another item during the lookup can place the generated PLU into the second item draft. The query excluded the first item's ID, not the second.

Suggested fix. Capture the draft ID/generation and apply the result only to the same still-open editor; disable or cancel stale generation requests.

Verify next. Delay the PLU lookup in an isolated fixture, open another draft, then complete it. The second draft must remain unchanged.

/attendance

src/lib/attendance.ts:82 · src/routes/attendance/+page.svelte:547 · src/lib/stores/mysql.ts:3728

P2

Repeated clicks can overlap saves and hardware tests

AUDIT-06 · Source + isolated logic

Items, Goods Menu, Suppliers and Tax Rates lack submission handler busy guards; draft controls remain actionable during awaited saves. Receipt/label/drawer test handlers also lack in-flight guards and their buttons are disabled by configuration only.

Impact. Repeated supplier saves append duplicate optimistic rows with the same ID; this does not prove duplicate persistent supplier records. Other saves can overlap and mutable drafts can change during commit. Hardware actions can send unintended repeated print jobs or drawer pulses.

Suggested fix. Capture an immutable submitted draft, guard handlers, disable edits/dismissal during commit and show Saving. Use a per-operation busy flag for printer/drawer tests.

Verify next. Double-click each operation against a delayed mock and assert one call/result. Physical drawer/printer behavior needs the intended Windows transport/hardware.

Retracted candidates—not bugs

These exclusions protect signature controls and prevent an incorrect image fix.

  • ADMIN-S05 — Delayed image upload can attach to a different item draft — The left-hand object reference of currentItem.image = await compressProductImage(file) is evaluated before await. The supplied image therefore writes to the captured old object, not a new currentItem draft. Image fetch in openEditModal already checks showModal/isEditing/currentItem.id. An async shared-error/toast cleanup may still be worth UX review but is not the claimed wrong-item image attachment.
  • ADMIN-S09 — ConfirmDialog dismissDisabled does not protect footer actions — dismissDisabled prevents generic dismissal, not intentional Accept/Reject choices. Checkout and customer-account signature dialogs set it true permanently while their footer must remain actionable. Secret removal already guards duplicate handler calls and disables its opener. Blindly disabling both footer buttons would break signature verification.

Final restoration/run status

CHECKOUT_EMPTY_AND_SYNCED; SANDBOX_PAYMENT_RESOLVED; MOCK_FIXTURES_DISCLOSED

After the user unlocked the Mac, the uncertain £1.69 sandbox attempt was reviewed through Reports > Payment checks. Check payment results refreshed provider state; Cancel expired test payment then canceled the uncaptured virtual intent and performed separated final provider checks. Tauri confirmed no unresolved payments remain. Read-only SQLite verification found the attempt cancelled with terminal Expired / payment intent Canceled, zero unresolved attempts, zero local terminal locks, zero online financial intents and zero offline uploads. The order count stayed 89: no canceled Dojo sale was added. Tauri is left signed in as Alan in Checkout mode, Synced, with zero trolley items and £0.00 total. The QA customer has zero money owed, zero loyalty points and is on hold. Initial inline Cancel Terminal failure remains a finding; the later guarded recovery PASS does not erase it. No product fixes or destructive history resets were made in this audit.

  • Checkout mode restored; Alan signed in; final main screen Synced with zero items and £0.00 total after unlocking.
  • QA customer: balance £0.00, points 0, Pay Later on hold; cash repayment and append-only history verified in Tauri and SQLite.
  • Daylight theme, Legacy POS crisp font, POS Extra large / Settings Normal, check-digit validation and original receipt layout restored.
  • Moved checkout controls and scale tiles moved back and saved; stock drafts cleared; customer-display window opened, live basket verified, then closed.
  • No pending online financial sale intent or offline upload at last read-only check. Pay Later recovery receipt 1000083 completed exactly once in the observed local database.
  • Dojo sandbox £1.69 attempt cancelled through normal guarded app recovery and separated provider verification. Tauri reports no unresolved payments, corroborated by SQLite. No associated sale was added: final order count 89, versus 86 before the three mock sales.
  • No product-source fixes, commits, pushes, cloud deployment, credentials changes or destructive cleanup were performed for this audit.
